## Oracle Seal Console  `oracle-seal-console`
*Category: oracle-console*

> A focused console for organizing an oracle request into a labeled "envelope reference" with a stable non-cryptographic checksum you can review before dispatch — honest that it does not encrypt anything.

**Purpose.** Oracle Seal Console is a developer/operator tool for preparing oracle request payloads before they're sent to the Morpheus oracle network. The user pastes a JSON request, tags it with a purpose and recipient, and the tool produces an "envelope reference" — a deterministic short checksum (e.g. 0x1a2b3c4d) that fingerprints the request so previews stay distinguishable and routable. It is explicitly NOT encryption: the copy repeatedly states the payload is "not sealed, hidden, or tamper-proof." A user opens it to sanity-check and fingerprint an oracle request locally, then copy the structured envelope payload for downstream use.

**Functional description.** No wallet connection is required (walletRequired: false). Flow: (1) The user picks a Purpose from a dropdown — three options: "Oracle input" (default), "Callback secret", "Attestation". (2) Types into a Recipient text field (placeholder "Enter recipient or oracle route") — optional. (3) Pastes JSON into the Request Payload (not encrypted) textarea (placeholder "Paste the JSON payload — NOT encrypted by this tool, kept as a plain reference"). (4) Clicks the primary "Build Reference" button (play icon). This runs LOCALLY — no network call — and computes two FNV-1a checksums: a payloadDigest (fingerprint of the payload text) and an overall digest from purpose|recipient|payloadDigest. The right column then fills with a result card. (5) "Reset" (rotate-ccw icon) clears the form and result back to defaults; the Network/Mode persist, the Envelopes counter returns to 0. (6) When a result exists, a "Copy" button (copy icon) copies the structured JSON payload to clipboard.

Result behavior: On valid JSON the status badge reads "Envelope preview ready", summary "{purpose} envelope reference prepared", and the hero "Envelopes" counter increments (1, 2, 3…). The result rows show: Protection = "Not encrypted — reference checksum only"; Purpose; Recipient (or an em-dash "—" if blank); "Payload is valid JSON" = Yes/No; Payload Checksum (0x…); Checksum (the overall 0x… digest). The full JSON envelope (kind: "oracle.seal.envelope", purpose, recipient, payloadValid, payloadDigest, envelopeVersion: "morpheus-seal-v1", digest) is collapsed behind a "Payload" disclosure (▾). On INVALID JSON: status becomes "Preview ready (payload is not valid JSON)", the "Payload is valid JSON" row shows "No", this is treated as a WARNING (no green success toast, the Envelopes counter does NOT increment, and the Checksum stat keeps its placeholder). There is NO live dispatch button here (no execute hook configured), so a standing notice reads "Preview only — dispatching requires a Morpheus runtime token."

**Interface description.** Light "Neo Soft" layout on a #f7f8fb page, centered max-width ~1080px. THREE stacked regions inside a vertical 16px grid:

1) HERO CARD (white, radius-lg, soft shadow, ~18px padding) — two columns. Left: a 48px circular soft-fill badge with a shield-check (lucide ShieldCheck) icon, then a copy stack: eyebrow "ORACLE REQUEST ENVELOPE REFERENCE" (11px/700 uppercase, .12em tracking, muted), an h2 title "Request Envelope Reference Builder" (~22px/800, -0.02em, ink), and a body paragraph "Organize request details into an envelope reference with a stable non-cryptographic checksum. The checksum is not encryption — the payload is not sealed, hidden, or tamper-proof." Right column: a vertical meta list of label/value pairs, labels in tiny uppercase eyebrow style, values bold tabular: "NETWORK Morpheus Mainnet", "MODE Envelope reference (not encrypted)", "ENVELOPES 0".

2) NOTICE STRIP (full-width white pill card, radius-lg, hairline border, xs shadow) — an info (i) icon in accent color + text "Preview only — dispatching requires a Morpheus runtime token." (13px, muted).

3) WORKSPACE — two columns (left ~0.95fr form, right ~1.05fr result, 16px gap, equal-height stretch). LEFT FORM CARD (white, radius-lg, 20px padding, 14px gap): label "Purpose" over a custom-chevron select showing "Oracle input"; label "Recipient" over a text input (placeholder "Enter recipient or oracle route"); label "Request Payload (not encrypted)" over a multi-line textarea; then an actions row — primary green "Build Reference" pill button (play icon) + ghost "Reset" pill button (rotate icon). RIGHT RESULT CARD (white, radius-lg, 20px padding): in the empty/first-run state it vertically centers a 48px circular soft-fill badge with a file-search icon, a small uppercase caption "RUN THE FORM TO PREVIEW THE REQUEST PAYLOAD." (deep-green), and a muted hint "The decoded request and its digest will appear here so you can review it before dispatch."

IDENTITY NOTE: the manifest declares an INDIGO accent (#4f46e5) intended to read as "private/sealed input" (used for the chrome: hero badge, eyebrow tints, status pill, empty-state icon, focus rings, notice icon), but the primary CTA renders in brand GREEN (#0a8050) per the shared NeoButton. In the current screenshot both the hero badge and empty-icon read as a soft indigo/violet circle. Form rows in the result use a 2-up grid of inset (#f7f8fa) tiles with uppercase micro-labels and bold tabular values.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / empty** — Hero card with shield badge + title 'Request Envelope Reference Builder', meta column (Network: Morpheus Mainnet, Mode: Envelope reference (not encrypted), Envelopes: 0); full-width 'Preview only — dispatching requires a Morpheus runtime token.' notice; left form with Purpose=Oracle input, empty Recipient + Payload, green 'Build Reference' + ghost 'Reset'; right card centered empty state — file-search circle icon, 'RUN THE FORM TO PREVIEW THE REQUEST PAYLOAD.' caption, hint about decoded request + digest.
- **Filled / ready to build** — Form populated: Purpose selected (e.g. Callback secret), Recipient typed (e.g. an oracle route), valid JSON pasted in the payload textarea. Right card still showing the empty state — used to show the moment just before the user clicks Build Reference.
- **Success / valid envelope** — Right result card filled: indigo/accent status badge 'Envelope preview ready' + summary '{purpose} envelope reference prepared', a 'Copy' button top-right; 2-up rows grid — Protection 'Not encrypted — reference checksum only', Purpose, Recipient (value or '—'), 'Payload is valid JSON: Yes', 'Payload Checksum: 0x…', 'Checksum: 0x…'; a collapsed 'PAYLOAD ▾' disclosure holding the formatted JSON envelope. Hero 'Envelopes' counter now reads 1+.
- **Warning / invalid JSON** — Same result layout but status badge reads 'Preview ready (payload is not valid JSON)', the 'Payload is valid JSON' row shows 'No', toast is a warning (not green success), the hero Envelopes counter does NOT increment, and the Checksum stat keeps its '—' placeholder. Designer should style this as a caution variant of the result card, not an error/destructive state.

**Visual identity.** Light Neo Soft surfaces (white cards on #f7f8fb). Signature accent is an intentional INDIGO/violet (#4f46e5, soft fill at 12% alpha) chosen to signal "private/sealed input" rather than the platform's default green or an amber warning — used on the hero shield-check badge, eyebrow/label tints, the status pill, the empty-state file-search badge, focus rings, and the info-notice icon. The primary CTA still renders in brand green (#0a8050). Signature visuals: the shield-check seal badge in the hero, and the file-search "review before dispatch" icon in the empty result card. Tone is honest/utilitarian: copy and the 'Protection: Not encrypted — reference checksum only' row deliberately undercut any false promise of encryption.

## Oracle VRF Console  `oracle-vrf-console`
*Category: oracle-console*

> A compact builder console for assembling Morpheus verifiable-randomness (VRF) requests — pick consumer, salt, proof mode, and round count, then preview the exact request payload and its client digest before you dispatch it on-chain.

**Purpose.** Oracle VRF Console is a developer/operator tool for planning verifiable randomness requests against the Morpheus VRF lane. A user opens it to compose a well-formed randomness request (who the consumer is, what salt scopes the round, single vs batch proof, how many rounds) and to inspect the exact JSON payload and its deterministic request ID/digest BEFORE submitting it to the Morpheus VRF lane or their own consumer contract. It is explicitly a preview/builder — the actual draw and proof verification happen on the VRF lane, not here. No wallet is required (walletRequired: false); the returned proof later matches back to the request id generated here.

**Functional description.** Single-tab ("VRF") console flow. The user fills four fields in the left command column: (1) Consumer — text, defaults to "miniapp-oracle-vrf-console", placeholder "MiniApp contract hash or app id"; (2) Salt — text, defaults to "vrf:miniapp-round", placeholder "Unique round or request salt"; (3) Rounds (1-10) — number, defaults to "1", out-of-range/non-integer entries are silently clamped to the 1-10 whole-number window; (4) Proof Mode — select with two options, "Single proof" (default) and "Batch proof". Primary action "Build VRF Request" (green filled, play icon) runs a local preview: it builds a deterministic request id digest (e.g. 0x1a2b3c4d) from consumer|salt|rounds|mode and renders the result in the right column. Secondary action "Reset" (ghost, rotate icon) clears fields back to defaults and empties the result. If consumer or salt is blank, the result shows a warning state ("Required fields missing" / "Enter a consumer and salt before building a VRF request.") rather than a payload. On success the right column shows a status pill "VRF request ready", a summary "{n} randomness round(s) prepared", a key/value rows table (Consumer, Salt, Rounds, an extra "Rounds adjusted: 2.5 -> 2 (1-10, whole number)" row only when clamping occurred, and Client Digest = the request id), a "Copy" button, and a collapsible "Payload" details block showing the full JSON ({ kind: "oracle.vrf.request", consumer, salt, rounds, mode, digest, client_digest }). Building a request also updates the hero "Request ID" stat and the sidebar (Network / Last Status / Request ID). A persistent info notice "Preview only — dispatching requires a Morpheus runtime token." sits between hero and workspace because no live execute hook is wired.

**Interface description.** Light Neo Soft layout, ~1200px, single green accent (#16c784). Top: a full-width white hero card (radius-lg, soft shadow, 24px padding) with a left intro block — a ~46px pastel mint icon badge (ShieldCheck shield-check glyph) + eyebrow "VERIFIABLE RANDOMNESS" (11px/700 uppercase, .12em tracking, muted) + title "VRF Request Builder" (~24-30px/800, -0.02em) + a 4-line gray description paragraph explaining the request id round-trips to the VRF lane. On the hero's right is a meta block of label/value pairs: "NETWORK Morpheus Mainnet" and "ENDPOINT Request builder (preview)" (the Requests tally is intentionally hidden via hideRequestCount). Below the hero, a full-width white pill/bar notice with an info (i) icon: "Preview only — dispatching requires a Morpheus runtime token." Then a 2-column workspace of two white cards. Left card = the form: stacked labeled fields (label 13px above each control) — Consumer (text input), Salt (text input), Rounds (1-10) (number input showing "1"), Proof Mode (select showing "Single proof" with chevron) — then an actions row: filled green "Build VRF Request" (play icon) + ghost "Reset" (rotate icon). Right card = the result/preview pane; in its empty state it is centered with a mint circular icon badge (file-search glyph), a soft green pill "Run the form to preview the request payload.", and a muted hint "The decoded request and its digest will appear here so you can review it before dispatch." Inputs use #f7f8fa fields with hairline borders and a green focus ring. There is also a left sidebar (host chrome) listing Network / Last Status / Request ID.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / empty** — Default field values pre-filled (Consumer miniapp-oracle-vrf-console, Salt vrf:miniapp-round, Rounds 1, Proof Mode Single proof). Right column shows the empty state: mint file-search icon badge, green pill 'Run the form to preview the request payload.', muted hint 'The decoded request and its digest will appear here so you can review it before dispatch.' Preview-only info notice visible above workspace. This is the current screenshot state.
- **Filled / ready to build** — User has edited Consumer (e.g. a 0x… contract hash), Salt, set Rounds to e.g. 3, Proof Mode = Batch proof. Form looks identical structurally; emphasis is on the active green 'Build VRF Request' CTA. Right column still empty until built.
- **Success / request ready** — Right card populated: status pill 'VRF request ready', bold summary '3 randomness round(s) prepared', rows table (Consumer, Salt, Rounds: 3, Client Digest: 0x…), a 'Copy' button top-right, and a collapsed 'Payload' details accordion that expands to show formatted JSON. Hero 'Request ID' stat and sidebar update with the digest.
- **Clamped-input variant** — Success state where Rounds was entered out of range (e.g. 50 or 2.5). Result includes an extra highlighted row 'Rounds adjusted: 50 -> 10 (1-10, whole number)' so the user sees their value was honestly corrected, with Rounds shown as the clamped value.
- **Validation warning (missing input)** — Consumer or Salt left blank then Build pressed: right card shows warning status 'Required fields missing' and summary 'Enter a consumer and salt before building a VRF request.', no rows table, host toast is a warning (not green success), digest/Request ID stat unchanged.

**Visual identity.** Neo Soft light theme with the default green brand accent #16c784 (mint #e4f8f0 badge fills, #0a8050 filled CTA). Signature visual is the oracle/verifiable-randomness motif: a mint ShieldCheck badge anchoring the hero (trust/proof), the file-search "decode the payload" icon in the empty result pane, and the manifest "dice" icon (category: oracle) signalling randomness. The personality is a precise, technical builder console — monospace-flavored digests (0x…), JSON payload preview, and honest preview-only framing — not a playful game.

## TrustAnchor Admin  `trustanchor-admin`
*Category: oracle-console*

> Operator command center for manually routing NEO across TrustAnchor's 21 AA agents and syncing their council votes.

**Purpose.** TrustAnchor Admin is a single-operator console for the back-office of a TrustAnchor abstract-account (AA) agent fleet. It does exactly three things: move whole NEO between the app's own 21 AA agents, change an agent's council-candidate target, and sync that vote on-chain. An operator opens it to rebalance NEO holdings between agents and steer where the fleet votes, because the candidate these agents vote for determines the GAS rewards TrustAnchor stakers receive. It is deliberately a read-only viewer for any wallet that is not the designated operator.

**Functional description.** Single overview tab, no mode switching. Flow: (1) Operator connects the designated TrustAnchor operator wallet — until then stats read zero and a caption "Selected route, agent count and reserves load once an operator connects." sits under the hero; a non-operator wallet gets a read-only banner and dimmed controls. (2) MOVE NEO card — operator enters "From agent" (1-21), "To agent" (1-21), and "NEO amount" (whole integers only; suffix "NEO"). A balance hint appears once the source agent's on-chain balance is known: "Move amount is checked against the source agent's on-chain NEO balance below. #1: 12 NEO". Validation blocks: same from/to agent ("Choose two different agents"), non-integer amount ("NEO is indivisible — enter a whole number"), and amount over balance shows a red alert "Amount exceeds the source agent's NEO balance." Primary CTA "Submit move" dispatches transferAgentNeo; toast "Agent transfer submitted". (3) UPDATE CANDIDATE card — "Agent" (1-21) + "Candidate public key" (placeholder "02...", must be a 33-byte compressed key). CTA "Update candidate" dispatches setAgentCandidate; toast "Candidate update submitted". (4) SYNC VOTE card — shows a "Current vote route" preview block, an "Agent" id field (auto-seeded to the live selected agent until the operator edits it), and a yellow witness note "Needs the agent witness — Syncing this vote requires agent #1 (NXa…b3f) to co-sign with its AA witness. If this session cannot provide that witness, the transaction will fail." CTA "Sync vote" dispatches voteAgent; toast "Vote sync submitted". Each CTA has an in-flight guard (shows loading, disabled) to prevent double-submit. Right rail is reference-only: a scrollable agent directory and a static operator-rule card; no actions there.

**Interface description.** Desktop ~1200px, two-column shell: left/main column ~0.78fr, right rail ~0.42fr (min 320px), 20px gap, 20px page padding. MAIN column top-to-bottom: (A) Hero card (white, radius 20, soft shadow, 24px pad) with a 52px circular soft-green badge (#e4f8f0 fill) holding a green gradient shield-with-check glyph; beside it an 11px uppercase .12em muted kicker "TRUSTANCHOR ADMIN", a 32px/800 -0.02em title "Move. Target. Vote.", and a 14px muted subtitle. Below: a 4-up stat row of inset #f7f8fa tiles (radius 16) each with an 11px uppercase muted label + 18px/800 tabular-nums value — SELECTED ROUTE (e.g. "None"), AGENTS ("21 / 21"), TRACKED NEO ("0.00 NEO"), RESERVE ("0.00 GAS"). When not live, a green-dotted caption line sits under the tiles. (B) Optional read-only role banner (warm #fff3dd, amber border) with eyebrow "READ-ONLY", bold "Operator wallet required", body text — only for non-operators. (C) Command grid: 3 equal NeoCards in a row (radius 20, white) titled "Move NEO", "Update candidate", "Sync vote", each with a 13px description, its fields, contextual hint/alert lines, and a full-width green primary button. Move card uses a 2-col field grid (NEO amount spans full width). RIGHT RAIL (stacked, 20px gap): (1) "Agent directory" card — section heading with a green-soft count pill ("21 / 21") at right; below, a 2-column scrollable grid (max-height 416px, thin green scrollbar, bottom white fade) of agent rows. Each row: "#3" id + bold balance "12 NEO" (or em-dash) + optional Active/Inactive flag, a truncated monospace account address, and "Candidate: 02ab…c4f1" or "No candidate". (2) "Operator rule" card — heading with a neutral uppercase "MANUAL ONLY" pill, a paragraph describing the 3 allowed actions, a causality line about GAS rewards, and a 3-item safety list: "No auto rebalance", "Candidate key is explicit", "Vote sync is a separate transaction". Mobile 390px: single column, hero → command cards stacked → directory → rule card.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / awaiting operator** — Wallet not yet connected (or operator not resolved). Hero stat tiles read None / 21 of 21 / 0.00 NEO / 0.00 GAS with the green-dotted caption 'Selected route, agent count and reserves load once an operator connects.' Agent directory shows a compact single placeholder (loading spinner state 'Loading on-chain agents…' or empty 'No AA agents loaded yet.' + hint). Command cards are visible but unprimed.
- **Read-only (non-operator)** — Amber 'Operator wallet required' banner above the command grid; the whole 3-card command grid dimmed to 0.72 opacity; CTAs disabled. Directory and operator-rule rail remain fully legible.
- **Filled / ready (operator connected)** — Live stats populated (e.g. Selected route '#7', 21/21 agents, tracked NEO and reserve figures), agent directory grid fully loaded with 21 rows showing balances/flags/candidates, Move card showing the source-balance hint, all three green CTAs enabled.
- **Validation / blocked** — Move card with same from/to or over-balance input: red alert 'Amount exceeds the source agent's NEO balance.' and disabled 'Submit move'; soft-green disabled CTA styling on cards waiting for required input.
- **Submitting (in-flight)** — A CTA in loading state (spinner, disabled) while transferAgentNeo / setAgentCandidate / voteAgent dispatches; double-submit guarded.
- **Sync-vote witness warning** — Sync vote card expanded with the 'Current vote route' preview and the yellow witness note explaining agent #N (NXa…b3f) must co-sign with its AA witness or the tx fails.
- **Success / toast** — Confirmation toast after each action: 'Agent transfer submitted', 'Candidate update submitted', or 'Vote sync submitted'; stats/directory refresh to reflect the change.

**Visual identity.** Neo Soft light theme with the standard green brand accent (no secondary theme). Decorative bright green #16c784 + soft-green #e4f8f0 chips/badges/count-pill; AA-safe filled CTAs use deeper #0a8050 (hover #086a45) white text; accent text on light surfaces uses darker #097d52; disabled CTAs use soft-green fill so they read as 'waiting on input' not broken. Inputs get a green focus ring (3px rgba(22,199,132,0.16)). Signature visual: a green-gradient (#4BE2A6→#16C784) rounded shield-with-checkmark badge in the hero. Single coherent accent discipline — the read-only/witness states intentionally break to amber/warm (#fff3dd, #b9791a) to signal caution, and the 'Manual only' pill stays neutral grey so it doesn't read as a third accent.

# Tools & Utilities

## Asset Factory  `asset-factory`
*Category: tool*

> A console for minting NEP-17 tokens from an audited on-chain template — configure supply, owner, treasury and mint policy, then sign or deploy a deterministic plan.

**Purpose.** Asset Factory is a focused, OneGate-loadable console for launching a NEP-17 fungible token through the platform's governed Factory contract. The user supplies only initialization parameters (name, symbol, decimals, supply, owner, treasury, mint policy) and pays only the Neo network fee — no NEF/manifest upload, no app fee. Deployment is only possible on a network where a factory admin has preloaded the template artifact; on every other network the console still produces a deterministic, wallet-signable plan the user can export as an issuer commitment or audit trail. A designer opens this to mock a trustworthy, "honesty-first" deployment workspace where the live preview, blocking-error checklist and artifact status always reflect what will actually happen on-chain.

**Functional description.** Single-screen "Factory" workspace (there is also an Activity tab). LEFT command column — one form card titled "NEP-17 Asset": (1) Network select (Neo N3 Testnet / Neo N3 Mainnet); (2) Name; (3) a two-field row Symbol + Decimals (number, min 0 max 8); (4) Initial supply (number); (5) Owner (placeholder "N...", auto-prefilled from connected wallet, with an inline "Use my address" affix when the field differs from the wallet); (6) Treasury (placeholder "N...", auto-mirrors Owner until edited); (7) a "Mintable after deploy" toggle; then a full-width primary green CTA "Generate template plan" (shows loading spinner while generating). Editing any field live-updates the preview and the package digest in real time. RIGHT state/output column, stacked cards: PREVIEW — a token "coin" tile showing the first letter of the symbol, the token name + symbol, and three stats Supply / Decimals / Mint policy (Mintable | Fixed supply), with hint "Live preview of what this template will create." TEMPLATE DEPLOYMENT PLAN card (variant flips white→green on ready / amber on blocked) showing Plan ID, full Plan digest, Template artifact status (Preloaded on-chain | Metadata only | Not registered | Unverified), optional "Estimated network fee ≈ {amount} GAS"; two sub-sections "Blocking errors" (e.g. "Symbol must be 2-12 uppercase letters…", "Initial supply must be greater than zero.") and "Warnings" (else "No blocking issues." / "No warnings."); a collapsible "View plan payload (JSON)" disclosure; and a 3-button action row — "Copy template plan" (secondary), "Sign template plan" (green/success, enabled only after a plan is generated and publishable), "Deploy via factory" (primary, enabled only when the artifact is preloaded and the exact plan hasn't already been deployed). Below the actions, conditional hints/results appear: "Generate a template plan first.", an execute-blocked reason, "already executed", a result block with Transaction txid + Deployed contract hash (copyable), the Wallet signature (copyable), or an error line. DEPLOY CHECKLIST card — a 4-step ordered list (Validate inputs → Select on-chain template → Deploy NEP-17 from template → Bind domain and catalog metadata), each step a status chip (Pending before generate; Ready / Manual / Blocked after) plus title + detail. ON-CHAIN CREATIONS card — a count bar ("{n} recorded on-chain" + Refresh) over loading/error/empty/list states; each list row shows Plan ID, a "Yours" tag when it's the user's, template ID + timestamp, and either a copyable deployed contract hash or "Record only — no contract deployed". ONEGATE LAUNCH URL card — the generated launch URL with a "Copy OneGate link" button and the honesty note about template-only deploys and network-fee-only cost. Primary action chain: pick network → fill token fields (or accept wallet-prefilled owner/treasury) → Generate template plan → review preview + blocking errors + checklist → Sign and/or Deploy via factory → see txid + deployed contract hash.

**Interface description.** Light Neo Soft console, ~1200px, single accent = mint/green #16c784. Top HERO band is one white rounded card (radius 20px, soft shadow) in a 2-column grid: LEFT a 48px mint icon badge (a ◆ diamond glyph, soft mint fill) + eyebrow "ASSET FACTORY" (11px/700 uppercase, .12em, muted) + H2 title "Asset Factory" (22px/800, -0.02em) + the long honest subtitle paragraph ("Configure a NEP-17 token from an audited on-chain template — supply, owner, treasury, and minting policy…"); RIGHT a subtle #f7f8fa inset status box (min 168px) holding a status PILL — DRAFT (neutral grey, first paint) / READY (mint #e4f8f0 bg, green text) / BLOCKED (red soft #fde7e9, red text) — plus a "DIGEST" eyebrow with the last-10 of the digest in bold tabular text, and a small network label ("Testnet"/"Mainnet"). Below the hero is a 2-column workspace: a single form card on the left, a stack of NeoCards on the right (Preview, Template deployment plan, Deploy checklist, On-chain creations, OneGate launch URL). Cards are white with hairline borders rgba(20,22,38,0.06), 14–20px radii, soft shadows; the deployment-plan card recolors to green-success or amber-warning to signal plan state. Components present: native select fields (network, in NFT/miniapp modes a template select), labeled text/number inputs, a checkbox toggle ("Mintable after deploy"), pill buttons (primary green filled, secondary, success green, ghost), monospace code chips for digests/hashes/txids/URL, a <details> JSON viewer with a <pre> payload, an ordered checklist with status chips, and StateView empty/loading/error states (the screenshot shows "No packages recorded on-chain yet."). Form labels and eyebrows are uppercase muted; stat figures and digests use tabular bold ink.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / draft** — Network=Testnet, empty or wallet-prefilled Owner/Treasury, Mintable toggle on. Hero pill = neutral grey DRAFT, digest shows a provisional last-10. Preview shows 'Untitled token' / 'SYMBOL' placeholder coin with — supply. Deployment-plan card is neutral white; checklist chips all read 'Pending'. On-chain creations empty state 'No packages recorded on-chain yet.'
- **Filled / ready to generate** — All token fields valid (e.g. Name, SYMBOL 2-12 uppercase, decimals 0-8, positive supply, valid N... owner/treasury). Live preview coin shows real name/symbol + Supply/Decimals/Mint policy. Generate button enabled; digest updates live as user types.
- **Plan generated — Ready** — After Generate: hero pill mint READY, deployment-plan card green-success. Shows Plan ID, full digest, Template artifact = 'Preloaded on-chain', 'No blocking issues.' / 'No warnings.', optional 'Estimated network fee ≈ X GAS'. Sign (green) and Deploy via factory (primary) enabled. Checklist chips Ready.
- **Plan generated — Blocked** — Validation fails (e.g. 'Symbol must be 2-12 uppercase letters or digits and start with a letter.', 'Initial supply must be greater than zero.') OR artifact 'Not registered'. Hero pill red BLOCKED, plan card amber-warning, blocking-error list populated, Deploy disabled with a blocked-reason hint; Sign may still be available for export.
- **Submitting** — Generate/Sign/Deploy button in loading state with spinner; other actions disabled during the in-flight dispatch.
- **Success / deployed** — Result block shows Transaction txid (code) and Deployed contract hash (code + copy), plus Wallet signature (code + copy) when signed. On-chain creations list now has a row with the new Plan ID, a 'Yours' tag, template ID + timestamp, and the copyable deployed hash.
- **Error** — Red error line under the actions (e.g. signing/execution failed, or 'This exact plan was already submitted…'); on-chain creations can also render its error StateView with a Retry button.

**Visual identity.** Neo Soft light theme with the default mint/green brand as the single accent (#16c784 decorative badges/chips, #0a8050 filled CTAs, #e4f8f0 soft-mint READY pill). Signature visual: the 48px mint diamond (◆) hero icon badge and the "DRAFT → READY → BLOCKED" status pill that mirrors plan validity; the Preview card's circular "coin" token tile showing the symbol's first letter is the app's identity motif. Honesty/audit tone reinforced by monospace digest/hash chips, the green/amber state-driven recoloring of the deployment-plan card, and red-soft blocking-error treatment.

## Gas Sponsor  `gas-sponsor`
*Category: tool*

> A free GAS faucet for new, low-balance Neo wallets — claim up to 0.1 GAS a day to cover transaction fees, then pay it forward by donating or sending GAS to others.

**Purpose.** Gas Sponsor solves the cold-start problem for new Neo users: you can't transact without GAS, but you need to transact to get GAS. It lets a wallet holding under 0.1 GAS claim free sponsored GAS (up to 0.1 GAS per day, resetting daily at midnight UTC) drawn from a community sponsorship pool. Once a user is funded, the same screen flips into a "Pay It Forward" role where they can donate back into the pool or send GAS peer-to-peer to help the next newcomer. A user opens it to bootstrap an empty wallet, or to top up the faucet pool after they've been helped.

**Functional description.** Primary flow (Request GAS): user connects a wallet -> the hero fuel gauge and three facts (GAS Balance, Tank Level, Remaining) populate, and the app checks eligibility (balance must be under 0.1 GAS). If eligible and daily quota remains, a "fuel-pump display" shows the chosen Request Amount in large green digits with "Max request" and "Remaining" sub-lines; user types an amount or taps a quick-amount chip (0.001 / 0.005 / 0.01 / 0.05), then taps the loud primary "Request GAS" (label reads "Connect wallet to request" until connected) -> a sponsored-GAS request is submitted, returning a request ID, and the quota strip below (a progress bar + "USED 0 / 0.1" + "RESETS AT —") advances. Ineligible/edge results swap the form for a centered message: "Not Eligible — Your GAS balance exceeds 0.1 GAS", "Daily quota exhausted — Please try again tomorrow", or "Sponsorship unavailable" when the faucet API is offline/unconfigured. Secondary flow (Pay It Forward): for an unfunded wallet this card shows a locked empty state ("Get funded first / Donate and Send move GAS you already hold. Request sponsored GAS above, then come back to help others."). Once the wallet has a GAS balance it unlocks two stacked forms — Donate (amount field with "Available: {x} GAS" hint and an optional sponsorship-pool address; routes GAS into the faucet pool; green/success "Donate" button) and Send GAS (recipient Neo N3 address + amount; a direct peer transfer that does NOT count toward the faucet; primary "Send" button). Both inputs validate against the live chain balance and only show errors after the user types.

**Interface description.** Single centered column (~880px max width, stacked cards, 16px gaps) on a light canvas — no two-column workspace. Top card is the HERO: a row with a 48px rounded green-soft badge holding a finance/trending-up icon, then title "Gas Sponsor" (22px/800, -0.02em) and subtitle "Get free GAS for transactions". Below it, inset on a #f7f8fa rounded panel, is the GAS GAUGE block: a left-side ~116x124px rounded "fuel tank" widget (1.5px green-tinted border, faint graduation-tick track, a bright green bottom-up fill with a 16% baseline floor, centered big tabular value "0.0000" + "GAS" label, a pill status chip below — green "NEEDS FUEL" when eligible / grey "TANK FULL" otherwise — and a percent/"Awaiting wallet" caption). To its right a definition list of three facts with 11px uppercase muted labels and 18px/800 ink values: GAS BALANCE, TANK LEVEL (%), REMAINING; under them a muted rule line "Free GAS is for new wallets holding under 0.1 GAS — up to 0.1 GAS per day." and, when disconnected, a green-soft note pill "Connect your wallet to check eligibility and your remaining allowance." Second card titled "Request GAS": a #f7f8fa "fuel-pump display" (uppercase "REQUEST AMOUNT" label, 28px green amount e.g. "0.01" + "GAS", and "Max request: 0.1000 GAS   Remaining: 0.1000 GAS"), an "Amount to request" number input, a row of four equal pill quick-amount chips (0.001 / 0.005 / 0.01 / 0.05; hover = green-soft), a full-width primary CTA, then a thin quota strip: 10px rounded progress bar + footer row "USED 0 / 0.1" (left) and "RESETS AT —" (right). Third card titled "Pay It Forward": either the locked empty state (56px green-soft circle with a social/wallet line icon, bold "Get funded first" heading, muted hint) or the unlocked dual forms separated by hairline dividers with uppercase section labels "DONATE GAS" / "SEND GAS". Identity is the green Neo Soft accent throughout (#16c784 fills, #0a8050 CTAs, #0b5e3e green text, #e4f8f0 soft chips); white cards, hairline borders, soft shadows, radius 12–20px, pill buttons/chips.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First run / disconnected (resting)** — Wallet not connected: hero gauge shows empty tank (0.0000, 16% baseline fill, green 'NEEDS FUEL' pill, 'Awaiting wallet' caption), all three facts at zero/0%/0.1000, the muted eligibility rule, and the green-soft 'Connect your wallet to check eligibility...' note. Request card shows defaults (pump display 0.01 GAS, max/remaining 0.1000) with the CTA reading 'Connect wallet to request'. Pay It Forward shows the locked 'Get funded first' empty state. This is the screenshot state.
- **Connected & eligible (ready to request)** — Low-balance connected wallet: tank partially filled to actual %, facts populated with real balance/tank level/remaining, green 'NEEDS FUEL' status. Request card active with editable amount, highlighted selected quick chip, loud green 'Request GAS' primary enabled; quota strip shows real used/limit and a reset time.
- **Requesting (submitting)** — Primary CTA in loading state showing 'Requesting...' with a spinner, controls disabled, optimistic feedback while the sponsorship request is in flight.
- **Request success** — Confirmation that sponsored GAS arrived (toast/inline 'GAS sponsored successfully!' / 'Request submitted! ID: {id}'), tank fill rises, GAS Balance/Tank Level update, and the quota bar advances toward 'USED 0.1 / 0.1'.
- **Ineligible / quota-exhausted / service-down** — Request form is replaced by a centered title+description block: 'Not Eligible — Your GAS balance exceeds 0.1 GAS', or 'Daily quota exhausted — Please try again tomorrow', or the amber service-state notice 'Sponsorship unavailable — service temporarily unavailable; you can still donate or send GAS below' (hero subtitle also switches to the offline faucet message).
- **Pay It Forward unlocked (funded wallet)** — For a funded wallet the third card shows the lead line 'You're funded...', then the Donate form (amount + 'Available: {x} GAS' hint + optional pool address, green Donate button) and the Send form (recipient Neo N3 address + amount, primary Send button), with inline validation errors appearing only after the user types.

**Visual identity.** Green Neo Soft default accent (#16c784 bright for the tank fill, badge, soft chips; #0a8050 filled CTAs; #0b5e3e deep green for accent text on soft fills; #e4f8f0 soft backgrounds). Signature visual is the vertical fuel-tank gauge: a rounded bordered tank body with graduation ticks and a bottom-up green liquid fill (with a glossy white meniscus and a baseline floor so an empty tank still reads as a gauge), paired with a fuel-pump-style request display showing big green pump digits. Reinforces the "fueling up your wallet" metaphor.

## Graveyard  `graveyard`
*Category: tool*

> Privately bury a memory on-chain — only its hash is anchored, with a permanent burial record and an optional paid "forget" ritual.

**Purpose.** Graveyard lets a user commit a private memory (a regret, secret, wish, or confession) to the blockchain without ever revealing its content: the text is hashed locally on-device (SHA-256) and only the hash is anchored on-chain alongside a category tag, for a small GAS burial fee. A user opens it to make a permanent, private, timestamped commitment to letting something go — and later, optionally, to perform a deliberate paid "forget" act or add a free epitaph note. It is framed as a somber digital-cemetery ritual ("R.I.P", "Burial Chamber", "Permanent record") rather than a utility, so the tone is reflective and intentional.

**Functional description.** PRIMARY FLOW (left "Burial Chamber" column): 1) Choose compose mode via a 2-tab pill toggle — "Write memory" (default) or "I have a hash". 2a) In Write mode: type into a "Your memory" textarea (placeholder "Write the memory to bury. It is hashed on your device — only the hash is written on-chain, never the text."); hint reassures the text stays local and only its SHA-256 is buried; as you type, a live "Hash (computed locally)" line appears showing a truncated preview (e.g. 12 chars…last 8). 2b) In Hash mode: paste into a "Content hash" input (placeholder "Enter encrypted content hash…"); a min-12-char rule shows an inline error "Enter at least 12 characters so the burial target is identifiable." until satisfied. 3) "Clear Hash" secondary button resets the input. 4) Pick a "Record tag" from 5 chips: Secret (default selected), Regret, Wish, Confession, Other — described as a category anchored on-chain with the hash. 5) Once a hash exists, a "Burial review" panel expands: a 3-tile grid (Hash quality with ✓/⚠ mark + status like "Ready for review"/"Hash too short"; Target = the hash preview; Wallet action = "Bury memory") plus a fee row (Record tag (local): selected tag · Burial fee: e.g. "0.10 GAS") and a sunk-fee note ("This fee is spent (not refundable)… no payout or reclaim."). 6) A persistent coral "Permanent record" warning explains burial is permanent and forgetting is an extra paid, irreversible state change. 7) The full-width primary CTA reads "Review burial" → on click becomes a "Confirm Burial" inline note ("Are you absolutely sure? The hash will be permanent.") with a Cancel button, and the CTA relabels to "Bury on-chain"; confirming triggers the wallet to deposit the burial fee in GAS and anchor the hash, CTA shows "Burying…". Invalid attempts trigger a shake animation on the CTA. SECONDARY FLOW (right "Burial Records" column): a refreshable, paginated list of this wallet's burials. Each record card shows an index number (01, 02…), truncated hash (10 chars…last 6), "tag · timestamp", a status badge ("Buried" green or "Forgotten" muted), and an optional epitaph line. Per-row actions: "Add epitaph"/"Edit epitaph" (free, opens an inline editor with a 120-char note, "Free — no deposit", Save/Cancel) and "Forget" (paid) which expands an inline confirmation showing the fee ("Forgetting costs {fee}…") + a reflective ritual note, with Cancel and a red "Confirm forget" button (forget fee is ~10× the burial fee). Pagination shows "Showing the most recent {shown} of {total} burials." with a "Show all records"/"Show fewer" toggle. Fireworks fire on a successful burial.

**Interface description.** Light Neo Soft theme, centered ~1140px max-width column on a near-white page. HERO BAND (white rounded card, soft shadow): left = a circular soft-mint icon badge (#e4f8f0 fill, green tombstone/headstone line glyph) next to a copy stack — tiny uppercase muted eyebrow "R.I.P", bold ~28px title "Graveyard", muted subtitle "Hash-based memory burial on-chain"; right = three inset stat tiles (#f7f8fa) with big tabular figures + uppercase muted captions: "0 / BURIED", "0 GAS / GAS SPENT ON BURIALS (EST.)", "0 / RECORDS". WORKSPACE = 2-column grid below. LEFT card "Burial Chamber" (bold card title): a pill segmented toggle ["Write memory" active in green text on soft fill | "I have a hash"]; a labeled textarea "Your memory" with placeholder + small muted hint beneath; a right-aligned "Clear Hash" pill (secondary, ghost-bordered); a "Record tag" field label over a wrap of 5 rounded chips (active chip = green outline + soft-mint fill + green text "Secret", others = hairline-bordered neutral pills); a muted helper line; the coral-tinted "Permanent record" warning box (light red/pink bg, bold title + body); then the full-width primary CTA "Review burial" — note: in the current screenshot the CTA renders as soft-green fill with green text (it should read as the main filled action #0a8050/white per the system). RIGHT card "Burial Records": header row with bold label + a count chip (0) + a "Refresh Records" ghost pill with a circular-arrow icon; body shows the empty state — a centered soft-mint circle icon (tombstone glyph) with bold "No burial records yet" and muted "Buried memories will appear here." When populated, the card becomes a vertical list of record cards (index numeral, mono hash, tag·time, green/muted status badge, optional epitaph, and ghost/secondary per-row action buttons), with inline expanding editor/confirm panels. Identity color is the standard Neo green accent; the only non-green note is the coral warning box. Radii 12–20px, hairline borders rgba(20,22,38,0.06), soft shadows.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First run / empty** — Hero with all-zero stats (0 BURIED / 0 GAS / 0 RECORDS). Left chamber in default Write-memory mode: empty textarea showing placeholder, Secret tag pre-selected, Permanent-record warning visible, no Burial-review panel yet (no hash), CTA 'Review burial' present but effectively disabled. Right card shows the empty state (tombstone icon + 'No burial records yet' + 'Buried memories will appear here.').
- **Composing / ready to review** — User has typed a memory (or pasted a valid 12+ char hash). The live 'Hash (computed locally)' preview line is shown; the 'Burial review' panel is expanded with the 3 tiles (Hash quality = green ✓ 'Ready for review', Target = hash preview, Wallet action = 'Bury memory'), the fee row (Record tag (local): Secret · Burial fee: 0.10 GAS), and the sunk-fee note. CTA active as 'Review burial'.
- **Hash too short / error** — Hash mode with a value under 12 chars: inline input error 'Enter at least 12 characters…', Hash-quality tile in blocked state (⚠ amber, 'Hash too short' + cautionary copy), CTA blocked; optional shake animation if the user attempts to proceed.
- **Confirm burial** — After clicking Review burial: inline 'Confirm Burial' status note ('Are you absolutely sure? The hash will be permanent.') with a Cancel button, and the primary CTA relabeled to 'Bury on-chain' (filled green).
- **Submitting / burying** — CTA in loading state showing 'Burying…', controls disabled while the wallet deposits the GAS fee and anchors the hash on-chain.
- **Records populated** — Right column lists burial cards: index 01/02…, mono hash (10…6), 'Secret · 2026-06-19 14:02', green 'Buried' badge, per-row 'Add epitaph' + 'Forget' ghost/secondary buttons; count chip shows N; refresh control present; pagination note 'Showing the most recent X of Y burials.' with Show all records toggle when truncated.
- **Forget confirmation (per row)** — A record card expands an inline confirm panel: fee line 'Forgetting costs 1 GAS. This records a paid state change on-chain and cannot be reversed.', a reflective ritual note, Cancel + red 'Confirm forget' button. After completion the card's badge flips to muted 'Forgotten'.
- **Epitaph editor (per row)** — Inline free editor on a record: labeled 'Epitaph' input (placeholder 'A short note for this memory (free, on-chain)', hint 'Free — no deposit', 120-char cap) with Cancel + 'Save epitaph' buttons; saved epitaph then renders as a small labeled line inside the card.
- **Success** — Burial succeeds: fireworks/celebration overlay, hero stats increment (Buried +1, GAS spent updates, Records +1), and the new record appears at the top of the Burial Records list with a 'Buried' badge.

**Visual identity.** Standard Neo green accent (bright #16c784 for the soft-mint icon badges/chips/fills #e4f8f0, deep #0b5e3e for green text, #0a8050 filled CTA). Signature visual = a digital-cemetery / memorial motif: a rounded tombstone/headstone line glyph in a soft-mint circle (used in the hero badge and the empty-state mascot), the somber "R.I.P" eyebrow, and "Burial Chamber"/"Burial Records" framing. One intentional non-green accent: a coral/light-red "Permanent record" warning box signaling irreversibility. Mono/tabular type for hashes and stat figures reinforces the on-chain audit-trail feel.

## MiniApp Factory  `miniapp-factory`
*Category: tool*

> Register a platform miniapp instance from a governed on-chain template — produce a deterministic registry record plus a catalog patch, optionally signed by your wallet, that an operator then wires and syncs to make the app launchable.

**Purpose.** MiniApp Factory is a OneGate-loadable developer console for spinning up a new platform miniapp from a vetted, on-chain template (reward vault, event ticket pass, soulbound certificate, or oracle console). The user fills a short parameter form, gets a live preview and a deterministic plan (with digest, blocking errors, and warnings), then registers it on-chain. The honest framing is central: the on-chain outcome is a registry RECORD + a catalog patch, NOT a deployed token contract — the instance is not launchable until an operator syncs the catalog patch and wires the underlying app. A developer opens it to mint a governed, auditable registration of their app idea and hand it off for go-live.

**Functional description.** Single-flow, command-on-left / state-on-right console. (1) Pick Network: a select with "Neo N3 Testnet" (default) / "Neo N3 Mainnet"; switching mainnet later surfaces a warning chip. (2) Fill the MiniApp Template form: MiniApp ID (text, must start with `miniapp-` and use lowercase slugs, e.g. `miniapp-my-launch`), MiniApp name (3–64 chars, e.g. "My Launch"), Template kind select with 4 options (Reward vault / Event ticket pass / Soulbound certificate / Oracle console), Admin address (Neo N3 address / Hash160, with a "Use my address" affix button that auto-prefills from the connected wallet), and two toggles on one row — "Needs oracle — adds a price/data feed" and "OneGate launch". The right column updates live as you type. (3) Press the full-width primary "Generate template plan" button (shows loading spinner while generating) — this validates inputs and stores the deterministic plan. (4) The "MiniApp instance record" card then shows Plan ID, full Plan digest, Template artifact status, optional Estimated network fee (≈ N GAS), plus two lists: Blocking errors (each a human-readable validation message, e.g. "MiniApp ID must start with miniapp- …") and Warnings (e.g. the always-present catalog-sync next-step note). A "View plan payload (JSON)" disclosure reveals the canonical JSON. (5) Three actions: "Copy template plan" (secondary), "Sign template plan" (green success button — signs the plan digest with the wallet as a portable developer commitment; only enabled once a plan is generated and publishable), and "Register on-chain" (primary; for the miniapp kind the label is "Register on-chain", disabled until publishable + execution available + not already executed). After execution it shows Transaction (txid code) and, where relevant, a wallet-signature block with copy. (6) Below: a "Registration checklist" — an ordered list of steps (Validate inputs, Select on-chain template, Create platform miniapp from template, Sync catalog patch to make it launchable) each with a status chip (Pending in draft, then Ready / Manual / Blocked after generate). (7) "On-chain creations" panel lists prior records (Plan ID, template ID, timestamp, "Yours" tag, deployed-hash-or-"Record only — no contract deployed" with copy), with a count bar, Refresh button, and loading/error/empty states. (8) "OneGate launch URL" card shows the generated URL with a Copy button and the honesty note explaining a record + patch is created, only Neo network fee is paid, and an operator must sync the patch + wire the app before it goes live.

**Interface description.** Light "Neo Soft" console on a ~1200px canvas. HERO BAND (top, white card): left side has a small mint diamond icon badge (◆) + eyebrow "MINIAPP FACTORY" (11px uppercase, tracked), title "MiniApp Factory" (24–30px/800, -0.02em), and a one-line subtitle explaining it writes an on-chain registry record + catalog patch, not a token contract. Right side is a status cluster: a state pill ("DRAFT" neutral grey / "Ready" green / "Blocked" red), a "DIGEST" eyebrow over the last-10 of the plan digest in tabular mono (e.g. `4d6b41e29`), and a small network label ("Testnet"). WORKSPACE = 2-column grid below the hero. LEFT COLUMN: one card titled "MiniApp Template" containing the form — Network select; MiniApp ID input; MiniApp name input; Template kind select; Admin input with an inline "Use my address" link-button; a 2-up toggle row ("Needs oracle — adds a price/data feed" and "OneGate launch"); then a full-width green primary "Generate template plan" button. RIGHT COLUMN (stacked cards): (a) "Preview" card — an avatar tile with the app's first initial + name + the `miniapp-…` id as a "symbol", and two stat rows "Template" (e.g. Reward vault) and "Services" (Oracle · OneGate, or None), with hint "Live preview of what this template will create."; (b) "MiniApp instance record" card (variant turns green=success when publishable, amber=warning when blocked) — a meta block (Plan ID, Plan digest, Template artifact status, Estimated network fee), a two-column alerts area "Blocking errors" / "Warnings" (each "No blocking issues." / "No warnings." or a bullet list), a "View plan payload (JSON)" collapsible with a mono <pre> block, then an action row: "Copy template plan" (secondary), "Sign template plan" (green), "Register on-chain" (primary); plus contextual hint/result/signature/error rows; (c) "Registration checklist" card — numbered step list, each row = a left status chip + bold step title + muted detail; chips read "Pending" in draft, otherwise Ready/Manual/Blocked; (d) "On-chain creations" card — count bar + Refresh, then list/loading/error/empty (empty shows "No packages recorded on-chain yet."); (e) "OneGate launch URL" card — the URL line, a Copy button, and a long muted honesty note. Cards are white with 16–20px radius, hairline borders, soft shadows; pill buttons; single green accent (#16c784 decorative, #0a8050 filled CTAs). Eyebrow/section labels uppercase 11px tracked; stat figures 18–24px/800 tabular-nums.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / empty draft** — Fresh load: hero shows neutral DRAFT pill and a placeholder/last-10 digest. Form is empty or wallet-prefilled (Admin = connected address). Preview shows 'Untitled miniapp' with placeholder id and Services 'None'. Instance record card is neutral (erobo) with provisional blocking errors, 'No warnings.' or the catalog note. Registration checklist chips all read 'Pending' (neutral palette, not a wall of red). On-chain creations shows the empty state 'No packages recorded on-chain yet.'
- **Filled / ready to generate** — User has typed a valid MiniApp ID (miniapp-my-launch), name (My Launch), chosen a Template kind, set Admin, toggled services. Preview updates live (initial avatar, template + services). 'Generate template plan' is enabled. Instance record still reflects the live preview; hero pill may still be DRAFT until generate.
- **Generating / submitting** — Loading state: 'Generate template plan' shows a spinner and is disabled; or after generate, 'Register on-chain' / 'Sign template plan' show loading spinners during dispatch. On-chain creations can be in its loading state ('Loading on-chain records…').
- **Plan generated — Ready** — Hero pill flips to green 'Ready', digest populated. Instance record card turns green (success) with Plan ID, full digest, Template artifact status, est. fee; Blocking errors = 'No blocking issues.'; Warnings shows the catalog-sync next-step note. Sign + Register buttons enabled. Registration checklist chips now Ready/Manual.
- **Plan blocked — validation errors** — Hero pill red 'Blocked'. Instance record card amber (warning) listing concrete blocking errors (e.g. 'MiniApp ID must start with miniapp- and use lowercase slugs.', 'Admin must be a Neo N3 address or Hash160.'). Register disabled; a checklist step shows a red 'Blocked' chip with its detail.
- **Registered — success result** — After 'Register on-chain': a result block shows Transaction (txid in mono code), optional deployed-contract / signature blocks with copy buttons, and the 'On-chain creations' list gains a new entry tagged 'Yours' marked 'Record only — no contract deployed'. OneGate honesty note reinforces the operator-sync next step.
- **Signed plan** — After 'Sign template plan': a 'Wallet signature' row appears with the signature in mono code + a Copy signature button; the JSON payload now embeds walletSignature. Hero/state otherwise Ready.
- **On-chain creations — error** — The deployments panel error state: 'Could not load on-chain records.' with a 'Retry' ghost button.

**Visual identity.** Default Neo Soft green identity (accent #16c784 decorative, #0a8050 filled CTAs, deep green text on light) with a mint-tinted hero icon badge — the signature visual is a small mint diamond glyph (◆) in a circular ns-badge--mint badge beside the title, paired with the developer-console two-column layout. The "instance record" card is state-tinted (green success when the plan is publishable / Ready, amber warning when Blocked, neutral white in draft), and the hero status pill (DRAFT grey → Ready green → Blocked red) plus the last-10 digest in tabular mono are the at-a-glance identity. Tone is honest/governed-tooling: muted explanatory copy emphasizing "record + catalog patch, not a contract."

## Neo Explorer  `explorer`
*Category: tool*

> A read-only Neo N3 block explorer: paste any tx hash, address, contract, or block and inspect it instantly — no wallet, no signing.

**Purpose.** Neo Explorer is a read-only lookup tool that gives a comprehensive view of the Neo N3 blockchain. A user opens it to paste a transaction hash, address, contract hash, or block number and immediately see a trimmed record of that item plus live network stats (block height, total transactions). It never requests a wallet connection or signature — it only reads public chain data — and links out to the full OneGate explorer for deep detail the in-app projection omits. Users also browse a lane of recent transactions and switch between Mainnet and Testnet.

**Functional description.** No wallet is needed (read-only). Primary flow: (1) the user picks a network with a two-option pill toggle — "Mainnet" (default, filled green, labeled "Production network") or "Testnet" ("Testing network"); (2) types or pastes a query into the single search field (placeholder "Search tx hash, address, or contract..."); (3) presses the full-width "Search" button or hits Enter. While the query is empty the button is disabled and the hint reads "Please enter a search query"; once text is entered the hint becomes "Paste any Neo N3 identifier and inspect it without signing." On submit, a spinner row shows "Searching..." (vs "Loading..." for the initial first-mount chain-stat fetch). The app classifies the identifier and renders a typed result card under a "Last lookup" heading: TRANSACTION (rows: Hash, Block, Time, Sender), BLOCK (Block index, Hash, Transactions, Time), ADDRESS (Address, Transactions count), or CONTRACT (Contract hash, Name, Methods, Update Counter, Indexed Calls). Each result card ends with a "View full record" link (external-link icon) deep-linking to the matching OneGate explorer page on the active network. Edge results: malformed input → "Unrecognized identifier" card with guidance; valid-but-missing → calm not-found notice ("Transaction not found on this network", "Block not found...", "No indexed activity for this address", or "Contract not found"). Secondary: a metrics strip shows the active network's live Block Height, Transactions, Recent TXs count, and Search Result status ("Result ready" / "None yet"); the Recent Transactions lane lists clickable tx cards (truncated hash + HALT/FAULT/Unknown state pill + timestamp), and clicking one re-runs a lookup (viewTx) for that hash. On-chain behavior: purely reads — no transactions are sent and nothing is signed.

**Interface description.** Single-column stacked layout (no two-column split), light theme on white cards over a subtle page. Four stacked sections: (1) HERO CARD — left circular mint badge reading "N3" (#e4f8f0 fill, deep-green text), then eyebrow "BROWSE NEO N3 BLOCKCHAIN DATA IN REAL-TIME" (11px/700 uppercase tracked), title "Neo Explorer" (~28px/800, -0.02em), body paragraph ("Explorer provides a comprehensive view of the Neo N3 blockchain. Search transactions, inspect addresses, and analyze smart contracts."), a hairline divider, then a facts row: a soft green pill "● Read-only chain lookup · Mainnet" (#e4f8f0 fill, dot bullet) next to plain text "Production network". (2) SEARCH CARD — header row with eyebrow "SEARCH TRANSACTIONS, ADDRESSES, CONTRACTS, AND BLOCKS" left and bold network name "Mainnet" right; a full-width rounded text input; a full-width "Search" button (currently a pale-green disabled state #e4f8f0 with green text, becomes solid #0a8050 white-text when active); a small status hint line ("Please enter a search query"); and a two-segment network toggle (pill group) where the active "Mainnet" is solid green and "Testnet" is an outlined/ghost pill. (3) METRICS STRIP — four equal inset tiles (#f7f8fa) each with an uppercase eyebrow label and a large tabular-nums value: "BLOCK HEIGHT", "TRANSACTIONS", "RECENT TXS", "SEARCH RESULT"; unloaded values show a calm "—". (4) RECENT TRANSACTIONS — section title "RECENT TRANSACTIONS"; in the empty state a card spanning two zones: left shows "NO RECENT TRANSACTIONS LOADED" eyebrow + bold "Run a search or refresh chain data to populate this activity lane.", right shows "SEARCH BY" with three pill chips "Tx hash", "Address", "Contract". When populated, each tx renders as a hoverable card: mono truncated hash + a state pill (green HALT / red FAULT / neutral Unknown) + right-aligned timestamp. Result cards (between metrics and recent) use label/value rows with mono values and a green "View full record ↗" link. Color identity is the standard Neo Soft green; rounded 12–20px cards with soft shadows; pill toggles/buttons at 9999px radius.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / empty (default)** — No query typed, Mainnet selected. Hero card, search card with disabled pale-green Search button + 'Please enter a search query' hint + Mainnet/Testnet toggle, metrics strip all showing '—' (Search Result = 'None yet'), and the Recent Transactions empty-state card with 'Run a search or refresh chain data...' plus the 'SEARCH BY: Tx hash / Address / Contract' chips. This matches the current screenshot.
- **Stats loaded, ready to search** — Initial chain fetch resolved: metrics strip shows live tabular figures (Block Height, Transactions, Recent TXs), Recent Transactions lane populated with hoverable tx cards (mono hash + HALT/FAULT/Unknown pill + timestamp). Query field still empty or filled; if text entered the hint switches to 'Paste any Neo N3 identifier and inspect it without signing.' and the Search button turns solid green.
- **Searching / loading** — In-flight state: search button shows loading; a centered spinner row with 'Searching...' appears below the metrics strip (or 'Loading...' on the very first mount before any user input).
- **Result — transaction/block/address/contract** — Successful lookup renders a 'Last lookup' result card with type-specific label/value rows (e.g. Transaction: Hash, Block, Time, Sender) in mono, ending with a green 'View full record ↗' external link. Metrics strip Search Result tile reads 'Result ready'. Designer should mock at least the transaction and contract variants (contract has 5 rows: Contract, Name, Methods, Update Counter, Indexed Calls).
- **Empty / not-found / unrecognized result** — Calm error cards: 'Unrecognized identifier' with 'That doesn’t look like a Neo N3 tx hash, address, or contract...' for malformed input; or a single muted not-found line ('Transaction not found on this network', 'No indexed activity for this address', 'Contract not found') for a valid-shaped but missing item — never blank rows.
- **Testnet mode** — Testnet toggle active (solid green Testnet pill), hero pill reads 'Read-only chain lookup · Testnet' with 'Testing network', search card header shows 'Testnet', and metrics reflect testnet figures; result 'View full record' links target the testnet OneGate explorer.

**Visual identity.** Standard Neo Soft green accent — single hue throughout: bright #16c784 for the mint badge/soft chips, soft fill #e4f8f0 for the 'Read-only chain lookup' pill and the disabled-search/active-network states, deep green #0a8050 for the active solid CTA/toggle, and #0b5e3e green text on the 'View full record' link. Signature visual cues: the circular mint 'N3' chain badge in the hero, the green/red/neutral VM-state pills (HALT/FAULT/Unknown) on transaction cards, monospace tabular figures for all chain data, and the calm '—' placeholder for unloaded stats. Light theme, white cards, #f7f8fa insets, hairline borders, soft shadows, pill-shaped toggles and buttons.

## Neo N3 Converter  `neo-convert`
*Category: tool*

> A client-side Neo N3 key toolkit: generate offline accounts, convert between WIF / private / public keys and addresses, and disassemble NeoVM scripts — all on-device.

**Purpose.** Neo N3 Converter (branded "Neo N3 Toolset") is a developer/power-user utility for working with Neo N3 keys and scripts without ever sending secrets to a server. A user opens it to (a) generate a brand-new account for cold storage and export it as a paper-wallet PDF, or (b) paste any Neo artifact — a WIF, a hex private/public key, a base58 address, or NeoVM verification-script hex — and instantly see every derived format (address, script hash big/little-endian, pubkey, WIF, disassembled opcodes). The single network-touching feature is an optional live NEO/GAS balance readout that appears only when a wallet is connected. The core promise is trust: "keys are never transmitted."

**Functional description.** Two intertwined flows live on one scrolling page (the manifest also declares Generate / Convert tabs).

GENERATE flow (hero card): User clicks the full-width primary CTA "Generate New Account". The app creates a Neo N3 keypair on-device and reveals a result block listing rows — Address, Public Key, Private Key (Hex), WIF — where the two sensitive rows are masked by default (rendered as "6chars********6chars"). Each visible row has a small ghost "Copy" button; copying flashes a green "Copied!" status. Two secondary buttons follow: "Show secrets" / "Hide secrets" toggle, and "Download Paper Wallet (PDF)" which is DISABLED until secrets are revealed (with the caption "Reveal secrets before exporting the WIF-backed paper wallet."). The PDF is a QR-backed offline backup. The hero also shows an "Accounts" stat counting how many accounts were generated this session (starts at 0).

CONVERT flow ("Convert Key" card): User pastes any artifact into a single text field (placeholder "Enter WIF, hex, or address...") and presses Convert (or Enter). The app auto-detects the format and shows a colored status banner naming what it found — "Detected: WIF" / "Detected: Neo N3 Address" / "Detected: Public Key" / "Detected: Private Key (Hex)" / "Detected: NeoVM Script" (green success) — or "Unknown format" / "Invalid format" (red error). Below the banner it renders only the rows it can derive: WIF input → Address, Public Key, Private Key, WIF; Address input → Address, Script Hash (0x, big-endian), Script Hash (little-endian); Public key → Address, Public Key; Private key → Address, Public Key, WIF, Private Key; Script → "Disassembled Opcodes" (a multiline monospace block). Sensitive rows are masked with a Show/Hide secrets toggle in the banner header; every row has a Copy button.

BALANCE flow (optional, hero): When no wallet is connected the hero shows only the Accounts stat plus the note "Connect a wallet to see live NEO / GAS balances." On connect, two extra stat tiles appear — "NEO Balance" and "GAS Balance" (read live via read-only RPC) — and the note swaps to "Balances are read live from a Neo RPC node — this is the only feature that uses the network." Disconnected balances show an em-dash "—", never a misleading "0".

On-chain behavior: none for key tooling (fully local crypto). The only network call is read-only RPC for balances. No transactions, no signing, no funds at risk.

**Interface description.** Light "Neo Soft" theme, green accent, three stacked white rounded cards (radius ~16-20px, hairline border rgba(20,22,38,0.06), soft shadow) on a single ~1200px column — currently NOT a 2-column workspace; everything stacks full-width.

CARD 1 — HERO (identity + generate): A 3-column header grid [badge | text | stats]. Left: a 48px soft-green rounded badge (#e4f8f0 fill, #0fb174 stroke) holding a padlock line-icon. Center: eyebrow "NEO N3 CONVERTER" (11px/700 uppercase, tracking .12em, dark green #076b44), title "Neo N3 Toolset" (24px/800, -0.02em ink), subtitle "Securely generate accounts and convert keys client-side." Right: stat tiles on #f7f8fa insets — "0 / ACCOUNTS" always present; "NEO BALANCE" + "GAS BALANCE" tiles appear only when connected (value 18px/800 tabular-nums, label 10px uppercase muted). Under the header: the muted balance note line. Then a full-width filled-green primary button "Generate New Account" (#0a8050, white text). After generation, an inset #f7f8fa result block holds masked key rows.

CARD 2 — "Convert Key": Card title, a 13px muted hint "Paste a WIF, private key, public key, or NeoVM script — everything is processed on your device.", a single full-width text input (placeholder "Enter WIF, hex, or address..."), and a full-width Convert button. In the screenshot the Convert button renders as the soft-green outlined/brand-soft variant (green text on #e4f8f0), not the deep-filled green — visually it reads secondary versus the hero CTA. Result appears as a tinted status banner (success = #e4f8f0 bg / green text; error = #fde7e9 bg / red text) with a header row (status label + optional Show secrets button) followed by result rows.

RESULT ROW component (shared by both flows): a white pill (#fff, hairline border, radius 12px) laid out [uppercase muted LABEL ~92px | monospace value, JetBrains Mono 12px, break-all, tabular-nums | ghost "Copy" button]. Opcodes use a scrollable multiline <pre> (max-height ~220px).

CARD 3 — "What you can paste" reference: a 2x2 grid of #f7f8fa tiles, each with a green uppercase label + gray description — WIF ("Wallet Import Format private key (starts with K/L)."), PRIVATE KEY ("64-character hex private key."), PUBLIC KEY ("Compressed public key — derives address and script hash."), NEOVM SCRIPT ("Verification script hex — disassembled into opcodes."). Below, a shield-icon trust note: "Key generation and conversion run entirely on your device — keys are never transmitted. The optional balance view makes read-only RPC calls when a wallet is connected."

Mobile (390px): cards stack; hero header collapses to badge+text with stat tiles spanning a full-width row; result rows collapse from 3-column to stacked label/value.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / resting (disconnected)** — Default state. Hero shows badge, 'Neo N3 Toolset' title, single 'ACCOUNTS = 0' stat, the 'Connect a wallet to see live NEO / GAS balances.' note, and the green 'Generate New Account' CTA. Convert card has empty input + Convert button, no status banner. Reference 'What you can paste' card visible at bottom with the trust note. No results anywhere.
- **Account generated (secrets hidden)** — Hero result block reveals 4 rows — Address & Public Key in clear monospace, Private Key (Hex) & WIF masked as '6chars********6chars'. 'Show secrets' button shown; 'Download Paper Wallet (PDF)' is DISABLED with caption 'Reveal secrets before exporting the WIF-backed paper wallet.' Each visible row has a Copy button.
- **Account generated (secrets revealed) + copy feedback** — Same block with all four values shown in full; button now reads 'Hide secrets', 'Download Paper Wallet (PDF)' is enabled. A green 'Copied!' status flashes after copying a row. Mock the full-length WIF/private-key strings wrapping (break-all).
- **Convert — success (e.g. WIF detected)** — Convert card shows a green status banner header 'Detected: WIF' with a 'Show secrets' button, followed by derived result rows (Address, Public Key, Private Key (Hex) masked, WIF masked), each with Copy. Show a second variant for Address input that yields 'Script Hash (0x, big-endian)' + 'Script Hash (little-endian)'.
- **Convert — script disassembly** — Status 'Detected: NeoVM Script' (green) over a single 'Disassembled Opcodes' multiline monospace block in a scrollable pre (e.g. PUSHINT8 / SYSCALL lines), with a Copy button. Demonstrates the tall scrolling output state.
- **Convert — error** — Red status banner 'Unknown format' (or 'Invalid format') with #fde7e9 background and red text, no result rows beneath — the input stays as typed so the user can correct it.
- **Connected wallet (balances live)** — Hero now shows three stat tiles: ACCOUNTS, NEO BALANCE, GAS BALANCE (real numbers, tabular-nums), and the note swaps to 'Balances are read live from a Neo RPC node — this is the only feature that uses the network.' Include a brief 'Loading balances...' spinner state at page bottom while fetching.

**Visual identity.** Default Neo Soft green identity — bright #16c784 / #0fb174 for decorative accents (the 48px soft-green padlock badge #e4f8f0, the green uppercase format labels, 'Copied!' status), #0a8050 for the filled 'Generate New Account' CTA, deep green #076b44 for the hero eyebrow (AA on soft-green). Signature visual: the padlock line-icon badge reinforcing the security/cold-storage promise, plus monospace (JetBrains Mono) key-value result pills and a shield-icon trust footer. Status uses semantic tints: success green #e4f8f0, error red #fde7e9/#ea3943. No secondary brand theme — this is a straight green tool app.

## Neo Name Service  `neo-ns`
*Category: tool*

> Search, register, and manage human-readable .neo domain names that map to your Neo wallet address.

**Purpose.** Neo Name Service (NNS) lets a user replace a long Neo N3 address with a memorable name like alice.neo, so people can pay or reach them at a readable handle. The user opens it to (1) look up whether a .neo name is available or who owns it, (2) register an available name by paying a GAS fee per year, and (3) manage names they already hold — point them at an address, transfer them, or renew before expiry. Each domain is an NFT the owner fully controls. Shorter names are "premium" and cost more.

**Functional description.** Primary flow: user types a candidate name into "Search Domain" (placeholder "myname.neo") and clicks "Search" (or presses Enter) -> a result strip appears with either a green "Available" status pill + "Cost: {N} GAS / year" + a "Register" button, or a red "Taken" status pill + the current "Owner" address. Clicking "Register" fires a paid wallet tx (button shows loading); on success the name is minted as an NFT (1-year term) and joins "My domains". Editing the search box clears the previous result/price (so cost only shows for the name actually searched). On the empty state, three example chips (alice.neo, wallet.neo, neo.neo) under a "Try an example" label are one-tap: tapping prefills the box AND runs the search.

Owned-domain flow: each owned name renders as a row with the name, "Expires: {date}" (or em-dash), and two actions — "Manage" and "Renew". "Renew" is a two-step confirm: clicking it fetches the renewal price then shows an inline prompt "Renew {name} for {cost} GAS (+1 year)?" with "Cancel" / "Confirm renew" (paid tx). A lapsed domain (expiry in the past) shows an "Expired" badge on the name plus a hint that renewing may no longer keep it (anyone can re-register).

Manage flow: clicking "Manage" replaces the domain list with a "Manage Domain: {name}" panel showing read-only info rows — "Current Owner", "Expiry Date", "Current Target" (or "Not Set") — plus two action groups: "Set Target Address" (input + button; validates Neo N3 address: starts with N, 34 chars, else inline error "Enter a valid Neo N3 address...") and "Transfer Domain" (receiver address input + button, same validation). A "Back to List" button returns to the list. Wallet connection is required for register/renew/manage actions; unconnected users can still search and see results.

**Interface description.** Single-column stack of three rounded white cards (~1200px desktop), no left/right split. (1) HERO CARD: left a 44px circular soft-green badge with a globe/meridian glyph (#16c784 on #e4f8f0); beside it eyebrow "NAME SERVICE" (11px uppercase tracked muted), title "Neo Name Service" (24-30px/800), subtitle "Human-readable .neo domain names for Neo addresses". Right side of the hero head holds three stat tiles in pill/rounded boxes: "0 DOMAINS", "0 EXPIRING", and a wallet-status tile "• Disconnected / WALLET" (gray dot when disconnected, green when connected). Below the head: a full-width search row — "Search Domain" label, large rounded input (placeholder "myname.neo"), and a filled deep-green "Search" button on the right. When a search runs, a result strip appears under the row (status pill + cost+Register, or owner). If connected, an "Address" bar shows the connected wallet. Errors render in a red banner.

(2) DOMAIN AREA CARD (resting/empty): centered empty state — a 48px circular soft-green globe badge, bold heading "You don't own any domains yet", a muted two-line hint ("Connect your wallet to see the domains you own, or search for a name above to claim one."), then "TRY AN EXAMPLE" label with three soft-green pill chips (alice.neo, wallet.neo, neo.neo) in mono-ish type. When the user owns names this card area becomes a list of per-domain rows (name + expiry + Manage/Renew). When managing, it becomes the "Manage Domain" panel (info rows + two input+button action groups).

(3) REFERENCE CARD "How .neo names work": a 2x2 grid of four inset (#f7f8fa) tiles, each with a small uppercase numbered label and one-line description — "1 · SEARCH / Look up any name to see if it is available or who holds it.", "2 · PRICE / Pay a GAS fee per year. Shorter names are premium and cost more.", "3 · OWN / Your domain is an NFT — point it at an address, transfer, or sell it.", "4 · RENEW / Renew before expiry to keep ownership; lapsed names can be re-claimed." Below the grid a footnote with a small globe icon: "Names map to wallet addresses, so people can pay you at alice.neo instead of a long address." This reference card hides while in the Manage panel.

Identity: standard Neo Soft green — bright #16c784 for badges/chips/icon fills, deep #0a8050 for the filled Search/Register/Confirm CTAs (white text), soft #e4f8f0 fills for the globe badges and example chips. Available = green status pill, Taken/Expired = red/coral pill+badge. Hairline borders, soft shadows, radius 12-20px, pill buttons.

**Key screens to mock.**
- **First-run / empty (disconnected)** — Hero with 0 DOMAINS / 0 EXPIRING / • Disconnected wallet tile, empty search box. Domain card shows the globe empty state 'You don't own any domains yet' + connect hint + three Try-an-example chips. Reference 'How .neo names work' 2x2 grid visible below.
- **Search result — available** — After searching a free name: result strip shows a green 'Available' status pill, 'Cost: {N} GAS / year', and a filled deep-green 'Register' button. (Premium short names show a higher cost.)
- **Search result — taken** — After searching a held name: result strip shows a red 'Taken' pill and an 'Owner' row with the holder's Neo address; no Register button.
- **Registering / submitting** — Register (or Search) button in loading state during the paid wallet tx; rest of UI stays put.
- **Owned domains list (connected)** — Hero stats updated (e.g. 3 DOMAINS, green Connected wallet, Address bar shown). Domain card becomes rows: name + 'Expires: {date}' + 'Manage' and 'Renew' buttons. Include one row in the renew-confirm state showing the inline prompt 'Renew {name} for {cost} GAS (+1 year)?' with Cancel / Confirm renew.
- **Expired domain** — A domain row with a red 'Expired' badge on the name and the muted lapsed-name hint warning that renewal may no longer keep it.
- **Manage Domain panel** — List replaced by 'Manage Domain: {name}' + Back to List; info rows Current Owner / Expiry Date / Current Target (or Not Set); two action groups — Set Target Address and Transfer Domain — each input + primary button, with an inline 'Enter a valid Neo N3 address' error variant. Reference card hidden.
- **Error** — Red error banner inside the hero card (e.g. 'Please connect your wallet first', 'Registration failed', 'Failed to check availability').

**Visual identity.** Neo Soft green default. Signature visual: the globe / meridian-lines glyph (a circle with curved longitude lines) used as the app icon, in the hero badge, the empty-state mascot badge, and the reference footnote — rendered as #16c784 stroke on a soft #e4f8f0 circle. Availability is color-coded: green "Available" pill vs red/coral "Taken" pill and "Expired" badge. Soft-green example name chips (alice.neo / wallet.neo / neo.neo) are a recognizable accent.
